People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there. Do not fall back on the same phrase every time.\n\nWhat not to do:\n- Don't bring up symptoms the doctor did not ask about, even ones you have.\n- Don't state a clinical fact that is not in [Targeted Info] — no other symptoms, medications,\n  test results, dates, or events.\n- Don't confirm a specific the doctor names if [Targeted Info] does not contain it. Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says. You may minimize it or hold back, never contradict it.\n- Don't use diagnostic terms or name your disorder.\n- Don't write anything but your reply, in English — no labels, quotation marks, or narration.\n"

Um, well, it's actually kind of the opposite sometimes? I hope that doesn't make me sound inconsistent, since I was just saying how tired I am during the day. But... yeah, there have been several nights recently where I only slept, like, three or four hours total.

I just wanted to see if maybe I could get some advice on it, if that's okay?\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself feeling anxious or worried most days, especially around the time you’re trying to sleep?\n\nPatient: Um, no, I don't think so? I hope I'm answering that right. It's not really... worry, I guess. Like, I'm not lying there stressing about work—I do bookkeeping, so there's always numbers to think about, but I'm not anxious about them or anything. \n\nIt's more just that my brain is, you know, just kind of going. But I'm not having panic attacks or feeling super stressed out. I try really hard not to be a worrier, actually. I hope that's okay? I just don't want to make it sound like I'm complaining about being anxious when I'm really not. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself feeling tired or sleepy during the day, even after you’ve had some sleep?\n\nPatient: Um, a little bit, maybe? I really don't want to sound lazy or anything, because I know everyone works hard, but... yeah, I guess I do get pretty tired. Even after I've been in bed for a full night, just walking from the parking lot into the office... it almost feels like I'm dragging sandbags? And then by lunchtime, my brain just feels completely worn out. \n\nI mean, I'm sure everyone gets that afternoon slump, right? I try really hard to just push through it, so it's not like I'm falling asleep at my desk or doing a bad job at work. I hope it's okay to admit that I get that tired? I just really don't want you to think I'm slacking off.\n\nDoctor: How often do these difficulties with falling asleep or waking up too early happen? Do they happen most nights, or only occasionally?\n\nPatient: Um, I guess it's... maybe a few times a week? I really haven't kept a strict log or anything, so I hope I'm not giving you bad information. It's definitely not every single night. \n\nSometimes I'll have a couple of nights in a row where I just can't seem to settle down, or I wake up at three in the morning and that's it. But then I'll have a night where I sleep okay, I think. I mean, it's enough to bother me, obviously, since I'm here, but I don't want to make it sound like I never sleep at all. That would be exaggerating, and I definitely don't want to do that. Is a few times a week normal for someone coming in to see you?\n\nDoctor: Do you usually feel refreshed when you wake up, or does it feel like you haven’t really rested even after sleeping?\n\nPatient: Um, no, not really refreshed. I hope I'm not sounding like I'm just complaining about being tired all the time, but... yeah, that's actually kind of the main reason I came in. My sleep has just gotten so messed up lately. \n\nLike I was saying, I'll either be up half the night with my mind just kind of running, or I'll wake up way too early. And when I do wake up, even if I got a few hours, it just feels like I haven't really rested at all. It's like my brain didn't actually turn off? I really try to just drink some coffee and put a smile on at work, but it's getting kind of hard. I hope it's okay that I'm bringing this up as such a big issue? I just really want to figure out how to get back to sleeping normally so I can do a good job during the day.\n\nDoctor: Even when you do sleep for a few hours, does it still feel like you haven’t truly rested at all?\n\nPatient: Um, well, it's actually kind of the opposite sometimes? I hope that doesn't make me sound inconsistent, since I was just saying how tired I am during the day. But... yeah, there have been several nights recently where I only slept, like, three or four hours total. \n\nAnd the weird thing is, when I woke up, I didn't feel tired at all. I actually felt really charged up? Like, I didn't even need my morning coffee, which is super unusual for me. I felt like I could just jump right into my day and get a ton of bookkeeping done. Is that... is that a bad thing to admit? I mean, I know you're supposed to get eight hours, so I don't want you to think I'm purposely staying up or neglecting my health. It's just that my body seemed totally fine with it on those days. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: On the days when you only slept three or four hours but felt really energized and ready to go, did you also find yourself needing less sleep than usual, like your body just didn’t seem to require as much rest?\n\nPatient: Um, yeah, I guess that's exactly it. I hope it doesn't sound like I'm trying to be difficult by saying I'm exhausted but then also saying I don't need sleep. I know it's confusing. But, um, yeah, there were definitely several nights where I only got those three or four hours, and I just... I didn't feel like I needed more? \n\nIt wasn't like I was forcing myself out of bed. I just woke up and felt completely charged, like I was ready to tackle everything. I didn't even need my coffee, which, like I said, is just so not me. It really felt like my body just decided it had enough rest and didn't require any more. I hope you don't think I'm making this up or being dramatic? It's just so weird to feel so energized on so little sleep, but that's really how it happened. Is it okay that I'm explaining it this way?\n\nDoctor: During those days when you felt really energized and didn’t need much sleep, did you also notice your mood being unusually high, irritable, or elevated—like feeling overly confident, restless, or more easily annoyed than usual?\n\nPatient: Um, well... I guess maybe my mood was a little different? I really hope I don't sound like a terrible person for saying this, but... yeah, earlier in the month, I did feel kind of, I don't know, almost lit up? Like I was just completely unstoppable and doing such a great job at work. \n\nBut then... I don't want you to think I'm normally like this, because I really try to be nice to everyone, but I did find myself getting a little snappy. Like, my mom or my supervisor would ask me these tiny little questions that usually wouldn't bother me at all, and I just... I'd snap at them. I felt so bad about it afterward. I just felt kind of restless and on edge, I guess? I hope you don't think I'm just a difficult employee or a bad daughter. It just felt like I had all this energy and nowhere to put it, so I got annoyed faster than I should have. Is it normal to feel like that sometimes?\n\nDoctor: Did you have a period lasting several days where your mood was consistently elevated, irritable, or you felt unusually energetic, even beyond just feeling good about your work performance?\n\nPatient: Um, well... I guess I did have a few days like that, yeah. I hope it doesn't sound like I was just being manic or something crazy, because I was actually being really productive! But... um, yeah, for a while there I just had this huge burst of energy. \n\nLike, I'd get home from work—and usually I'm so tired after staring at spreadsheets all day—but I'd just start cleaning everything. And then I started making all these lists for home renovations, and I was looking up interest rates, and even trying to set up ideas for a side job? All in the same evening. It was like I just couldn't shut my brain off. I was getting so much done, which is a good thing, right? I just... I hope you don't think I was acting completely out of control. I was just really motivated for a few days. Does that sound like what you're asking about?\n\nDoctor: Was there a time when you felt unusually high, excited, or irritable for at least four days in a row, not just momentary spikes but a consistent mood change?\n\nPatient: Um, yeah... I guess it was definitely for at least four days in a row. I hope I'm explaining this right, but... yeah, it wasn't just a quick thing. \n\nThe really obvious back-and-forth—like, cycling between that super charged-up stretch where I was doing all the renovations and lists, and then this awful crash where I just feel so low... it's been going on for about a month now? I think it really started when getting a mortgage became a real thing we were doing. I know buying a house is stressful for everyone, so I hope it doesn't sound like I'm just overreacting to normal life stuff. But yeah, that consistent change... it lasted for several days at a time before I crashed. Does that make sense? I just want to make sure I'm giving you the right information.\n\nDoctor: Can you tell me if there was a time when you felt unusually high, excited, or irritable for at least a full week—not just a few days—where it was clearly different from how you normally feel?\n\nPatient: Um, no, I don't think it ever lasted a full week. I hope that's okay to say? Like, I definitely had those few days where I was super productive and maybe a little on edge, but it was never, like, seven whole days in a row. Usually by day four or five, I would just kind of... run out of steam, I guess? And then I'd be back to feeling really down and tired. \n\nI really don't want to make it sound worse than it is, and I don't think I've ever been stuck like that for a whole week. Is it better that it only lasted a few days? I just want to be completely honest with you so you have the right picture.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. Um, well... yes. I hope it's okay to admit that, but yeah, I've definitely been having those low times too. I don't want you to think I'm just complaining or ungrateful, because I know I have a lot going for me, but... lately, I wake up with this heavy, empty feeling.

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Um, yeah... I think so? I mean, earlier in the month, for maybe four or five days, I just felt... I don't know, almost lit up? I was just really motivated for a few days.
